# Springboard scouting: start here

**What this is:** the author's request of 2026-11-15: go through every represented nation one at a time, each sign one at a time, with one subagent per concept, and write **springboard sheets**: surface data and "jumping-off points" for where to begin looking. **It is not research**; real research is a later task (about the next 6 to 10 months). The method is `<DM>/Shared/Springboard_Scouting.md`; the agent brief is `<DM>/_dispatch/SPRINGBOARD_BRIEF.md`; the sheet template is `<DM>/Templates/T_Springboard.md`.

`<DM>` = `/home/kuroskalacs/Documents/Doll-Fi/media/Reference/DerivationMechanisms/Environment/DCI [Deep Culture Investigation]`
`<RESULTS>` = `/home/kuroskalacs/Documents/Doll-Fi/media/Reference/CultureLibrary/Springboarding`

**Scale:** 84 nations × 130 sheets (118 concepts + 12 sign domains) = **10,920 sheets**. A sheet is about 250 to 450 words, written from the agent's own knowledge, no web.

**Output:** `<RESULTS>/Targets/<region>/<nation>/Springboards/NN - <Sign>/` (`00 - Domain and folkways.md`, `NN - <Concept>.md`).

**Status grid (read from the files):** `python3 -I "<DM>/_dispatch/springboard_status.py"` (all nations with any sheet) or `... springboard_status.py <Nation>` (per sign, with counts of done and in progress).

## Order
- **Nations, one at a time, in the master tracker's order:** Cascadia, Alaska, Sonora (the first novels), then Korea, Sinian Federation, Japan, and so on down the table.
- **Within a nation, signs one at a time, Aries to Pisces.** Within a sign, the domain and all concepts run in parallel (10 to 13 agents).

## Per-sign procedure (the run agent, or the main session, does this)
1. `python3 -I "<DM>/_dispatch/springboard_status.py" <Nation>` to find the first sign not complete.
2. List its jobs: `python3 -I "<DM>/_dispatch/concept_block.py" list <sign number>` (line 0 is the domain).
3. For each job not already `STATUS: done`, launch one agent with: *"Read and follow the brief `<DM>/_dispatch/SPRINGBOARD_BRIEF.md` exactly. Your job: NATION = <Nation>. SIGN = <n>, <Sign>. CONCEPT = <k> (<name>). Scouting only: no web, no research. Reply with one line."* A file whose first line says `in progress` is relaunched with the same prompt (the brief says to overwrite it).
4. When all return, re-run the status script. If any sheet is missing or not `done`, relaunch just those once.
5. Spot-check two sheets from the sign against the rules (below), then move on to the next sign.

## Pilot result (2026-11-15, Cascadia × Aries, 11 sheets)
- **Quality: good.** Real, well-known sources, everything marked *check*, no findings, standing rules mostly held (South Asian pointers set aside under "held for review"). Fixed: the brief's folder lookup (it missed most nations), a South Asian community named under "who to compare" (now forbidden outside the held line), and the length target (sheets run 700 to 900 words counting markup; the spec now says so).
- **Cost: about 57,000 tokens per agent, about 640,000 per nation × sign** (11 agents), because every agent re-reads the spec, brief and avenue block. At that rate the full 10,920 sheets would be about 640 million tokens. A cheaper shape (one agent per nation × sign writing all 10 to 12 sheets) would be about a tenth of that. **The author decides before anything beyond the pilot runs.**

## Spot-check rules (what a bad sheet looks like)
- **Findings** (statements about how the culture understands the concept): rewrite as a search term or cut.
- **Invented names:** a specific title, author, URL, statistic or institution that does not look real. Replace with the source *type* and a query.
- **Unmarked names:** anything named without *check*.
- **Standing-rule breaks:** "Indian" (other than a published title), South Asian pointers outside `held_for_review`, law outside Libra and Capricorn, national stereotypes, zodiac terms as pointers.
- **Too long or padded:** over about 450 words (650 for a domain).


## Overnight test day: Cascadia, signs 4 to 12 (author's go, 2026-11-15; weekly allotment at 2% when started)
**Goal:** finish Cascadia (Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius, Pisces), per-concept agents, nation-level, scouting only. The author reads a full report in the morning: `reports/2026-11-15_Cascadia_test_day_report.md`. The point of the day is also to measure the real cost of a nation-day (about 5.8 million tokens expected: 9 signs, about 97 agents).

**Run agent prompt (saved so a cutoff loses nothing):**
> You are the run agent for DCI springboard scouting. Your nation is **Cascadia**, signs **4 (Cancer) to 12 (Pisces)**; signs 1-3 are done. Read `/home/kuroskalacs/Documents/Doll-Fi/media/Literature/books/CurrentNovelDocs/worldbuilding/Deep_Culture_Investigation/_dispatch/SPRINGBOARD_PROGRESS.md` (this file) and the method `<DM>/Shared/Springboard_Scouting.md`. `<DM>` = `/home/kuroskalacs/Documents/Doll-Fi/media/Reference/DerivationMechanisms/Environment/DCI [Deep Culture Investigation]`. Chain everything yourself; no one is watching.
> **For each sign, in order, one at a time:**
> 1. List its jobs: `python3 -I "<DM>/_dispatch/concept_block.py" list <n>` (line 0 is the domain).
> 2. Launch one sub-agent per job (domain plus every concept) **all in one message, in the background**, each with exactly this prompt (fill the braces): *"Read and follow the brief `<DM>/_dispatch/SPRINGBOARD_BRIEF.md` exactly. Your job: NATION = Cascadia. SIGN = {n}, {Sign}. CONCEPT = {k} ({name}). Scouting only: no web, no research. Aim for about 750 words counting table markup; never over 1,000. Reply with one line."* If you cannot launch sub-agents, write the sheets yourself one at a time, following the brief exactly.
> 3. Wait for all to finish. Then run `python3 -I "<DM>/_dispatch/springboard_status.py" Cascadia` and `python3 -I "<DM>/_dispatch/springboard_qa.py" Cascadia <n>`.
> 4. Any sheet missing, not `STATUS: done`, or flagged by the QA: relaunch that one job once, adding the problem to its prompt. If it still fails, fix it by hand with the smallest edit (a South Asian line becomes the category-only held line; a URL is removed; a finding sentence becomes a search term or is cut). Log what you did.
> 5. Append one line per sign to the Run log in this file: the sheets done, agents launched, relaunches, QA problems found and how they were fixed, the average and longest word count (`wc -w`).
> 6. Only then start the next sign. Never skip a sign.
> **When all nine are done:** run the QA for all of Cascadia; then write the morning report to `<RESULTS>/_dispatch/reports/2026-11-15_Cascadia_test_day_report.md` (RESULTS = `/home/kuroskalacs/Documents/Doll-Fi/media/Literature/books/CurrentNovelDocs/worldbuilding/Deep_Culture_Investigation`): per-sign table (sheets, agents launched, relaunches, QA problems, avg and max words); totals (sheets, agents, wall-clock from your first launch to your last); every rule break and how fixed; anything odd (a sheet that read like research, an invented-looking name, a thin concept); what you would change in the brief; any question for the author. Touch only the Springboards folders for Cascadia, this file's Run log, and the report. If the usage window cuts you off, the status script resumes whoever continues.

## Cheap shape (author's plan of 2026-11-15; pilot after 15:00 on Alaska)
**Why:** the per-concept shape cost about 61,000 tokens per sheet, mostly fixed reading. The author wants the whole survey done fast (one or two nations a day, about 6 weeks to 3 months), and the meter is shared with the ULM on cities, which has priority. **One agent per nation-sign** (writing the domain and every concept of that sign, one sheet at a time) should cost about a tenth. Untested.

**Clean-measurement rule:** run the pilot **after 15:00**, when the ULM is not running; the author reads the meter just before and just after. Alaska is a light test (few units, documentation thin), so it will under-measure a typical nation: a second pilot on a mid-size nation (Sonora or Korea) sets the pace. Cluster agents (see `SPRINGBOARD_CLUSTERS.md`) are measured on the first cluster.

**Pilot run agent prompt (launch as ONE background agent; chain every step inside it):**
> You are the run agent for DCI springboard scouting, cheap shape. Nation: **Alaska**, all twelve signs, Aries to Pisces. Read `<RESULTS>/_dispatch/SPRINGBOARD_PROGRESS.md` (this file) and the method `<DM>/Shared/Springboard_Scouting.md`.
> For each sign, in order, one at a time: (1) `python3 -I "<DM>/_dispatch/springboard_status.py" Alaska` to find the first incomplete sign; (2) launch **one** sub-agent for the sign (in the background), with exactly this prompt (fill the braces): *"Read and follow the brief `<DM>/_dispatch/SPRINGBOARD_BRIEF.md` exactly. NATION = Alaska, SIGN = {n}, {Sign}. Get the job list with `python3 -I "<DM>/_dispatch/concept_block.py" list {n}` (line 0 is the domain), and write **every** job's sheet, one at a time, each exactly as the brief says for a single concept (its own `concept_block.py` call, its own STATUS line written last, 750 words by `wc`, never over 1,000). Each sheet is written from scratch for Alaska: do not reuse sentences between sheets. Scouting only: no web, no research. Reply with one line."*; (3) when it returns, run `springboard_status.py Alaska` and `springboard_qa.py Alaska {n}`; (4) relaunch any missing, not-done or QA-flagged sheet as a single-concept job with the problem added; fix by the smallest edit if it fails twice; (5) append one Run log line per sign (sheets, agents, relaunches, QA problems and fixes, avg and max words); (6) go to the next sign. Never skip a sign. When all twelve are done, write a short report to `<RESULTS>/_dispatch/reports/2026-11-15_Alaska_cheap_shape_report.md`: per-sign table, rule breaks and fixes, and the signs where the sheets read thinner, more repetitive or less specific than Cascadia's (name the sheets). Touch only Alaska's Springboards folders, this file's Run log and the report.

**Quality comparison (main session, after the run):** read the same concept from Cascadia and Alaska for four signs; check *check*-marking, invented names, findings, repeated sentences across sheets in a sign (`diff`/shared-line count), and word counts. If sheets blur or repeat, use the middle shape (two or three concepts per agent).

## Resuming after a cutoff
The files are the truth. Run the status script, find the first nation with an incomplete sign, and continue from step 1. No timer or session state is needed.

## Run agent prompt (for an unattended run; launch as ONE background agent, and chain every step inside it)
> You are the run agent for DCI springboard scouting. Read `<RESULTS>/_dispatch/SPRINGBOARD_PROGRESS.md` (this file), then follow "Per-sign procedure" for the nation(s) the author named: <NATIONS>. Work sign by sign, Aries to Pisces, launching each sign's agents together, waiting for them, checking, and logging a line in the Run log for each sign. Never skip a sign; never launch the next nation until the current one has all twelve signs `done`. Touch only the Springboards folders and this file's Run log. If the usage window cuts you off, the status script resumes you. Stop after <NATIONS> and report: sheets done, sheets relaunched, rule breaks found, anything the author should know.

## Author's plan idea (2026-11-15), not yet decided
Pause the ULM city runs for about a week and run springboards for **all remaining nations in one go** (one nation at a time, clusters sharing agents), then spend **1 to 3 days per nation over the following months** on full-depth research, starting from each springboard sheet and following every lead, to get a complete model per nation. **Decision waits on four measurements:** (1) Sonora and Korea cost in the cheap shape (clean meter reading after 15:00); (2) the first cluster's cost (Nordic, proposed); (3) the Cascadia Stage 1 research run (about 495 searches, set up in `STAGE1_RUN_PROGRESS.md`) as the cost sample for deep research per nation; (4) whether quality holds without blurring. Alaska (light nation): about 11,200 tokens per sheet, about 1.6 million per nation, 66 minutes wall-clock.

### Prompt lever for the next nations (from the Korea report)
Add to the per-sign sub-agent prompt: *"Every sheet needs at least one pre-modern or classical pointer, at least one pointer for an internal region or minority other than the two or three the nation is best known for (do not name the same two comparison units on every sheet), and the nation's native-script forms (for example hangul plus Hanja, Cyrillic, Arabic script) in the search terms, marked check where unsure."* Korea's run showed 1.25 to 1.5% of the weekly allotment per nation (author's reading); the three nations measured (Alaska, Sonora, Korea) cost 1.45 to 1.57 million sub-agent tokens each, so the per-nation cost looks stable.

## THE FULL RUN: author's ruling and plan (2026-11-15, evening) — start here for the run
**Rulings:**
1. **No cluster savings.** Every nation runs on its own: **one agent per nation-sign** (the "cheap shape" is just the process version), 12 sub-agents per nation. `SPRINGBOARD_CLUSTERS.md` is shelved.
2. **Accuracy and depth come before token economy.** Written into `<DM>/_dispatch/SPRINGBOARD_BRIEF.md` ("Priority" block; length target now 700 to 1,000 words per concept sheet, domain up to 1,200, 12 to 16 distinct pointers; full reads of every input; native-script and minority-language terms; pre-modern pointers; internal-region depth) and in the spec. The measurements so far (Alaska, Sonora, Korea: 1.45 to 1.57 million sub-agent tokens per nation, 1.25 to 1.5% of the weekly allotment per the author's readings) were taken under the old, cost-minimizing brief, so **the first nations under the new brief must be re-measured**: expect a higher cost.
3. **Pace:** this week **3 to 4 nations a day** (finish the ULM run now underway, possibly one more); **next week 7 to 8 nations a day with ALL ULM runs paused for the full week**, to finish every remaining nation's springboards.

**Arithmetic (80 nations left after Cascadia, Alaska, Sonora, Korea):** this week 3 to 4 a day for 7 days = 21 to 28; next week 7 to 8 a day = 49 to 56; together 70 to 84, so 4 a day this week and 8 a day next is needed to reach 80. At 1.25 to 1.5% each: this week 35 to 42% of the allotment, next week 65 to 78% (more if the deeper brief raises the cost by a third: about 85 to 100%, which leaves almost no slack). Re-measure after the first day, and adjust the daily count to what the meter shows.

**Order:** the master tracker's order (by census size): 5 Sinian Federation, 6 Japan, 7 Australia, 8 CSA, 9 United Kingdom, 10 Indonesia, 11 Germany, 12 Brazil, 13 France, 14 Appalachia, 15 New England, 16 Austro-Bavaria, and so on down the table (Greenland, 84, last).

**Running several nations at once (3 to 8 a day):** launch **one run agent per nation, all at once, in the background**; each runs its nation's 12 signs one after another (a sign's single sub-agent at a time), so at most as many sub-agents run at once as nations are in flight. Run agents **append** their Run log lines with `>>` and must **not** touch this file's first line or any other nation's folder; the main session updates the status line and the checklist.

**Per-nation run-agent prompt (fill {NATION}; saved so a cutoff loses nothing):**
> You are the run agent for DCI springboard scouting. Nation: **{NATION}**, all twelve signs, Aries to Pisces. Read `<RESULTS>/_dispatch/SPRINGBOARD_PROGRESS.md` (sections "Cheap shape" and "THE FULL RUN") and the method `<DM>/Shared/Springboard_Scouting.md`; `<RESULTS>` = /home/kuroskalacs/Documents/Doll-Fi/media/Reference/CultureLibrary/Springboarding and `<DM>` = /home/kuroskalacs/Documents/Doll-Fi/media/Reference/DerivationMechanisms/Environment/DCI [Deep Culture Investigation]. Chain everything yourself. **Accuracy and depth come before token economy** (the brief's Priority block). For each sign in order: (1) `python3 -I "<DM>/_dispatch/springboard_status.py" {NATION}` for the first incomplete sign; (2) launch ONE sub-agent (background) with: "Read and follow the brief <DM>/_dispatch/SPRINGBOARD_BRIEF.md exactly, including its Priority block and its rule about a missing scope file. NATION = {NATION}, SIGN = {n}, {Sign}. Get the job list with `python3 -I "<DM>/_dispatch/concept_block.py" list {n}` (line 0 is the domain) and write EVERY job's sheet, one at a time, each exactly as the brief says for a single concept: read that concept's whole block (no cutoffs), write that sheet, then the next. Each sheet is written from scratch for {NATION}: no reused sentences or table rows, varied sources, 12 to 16 distinct pointers, the nation's native-script and minority-language terms, at least one pre-modern pointer where the culture has one, pointers for internal regions and minorities beyond the best-known two or three. Scouting only: no web, no research. Reply with one line."; (3) when it returns, run `springboard_status.py {NATION}` and `python3 -I "<DM>/_dispatch/springboard_qa.py" {NATION} {n}`; (4) relaunch any missing, not-done or QA-flagged sheet as a single-concept job with the problem added; fix by the smallest edit if it fails twice (a published title or an organization's own name containing "Indian" is a false positive: keep it as published, in quotation marks); (5) append one Run log line per sign with `>>` (sheets, agents, relaunches, QA problems and fixes, average and longest word count by `wc -w`, the sub-agent's token usage, the time); (6) go on. Never skip a sign. When all twelve are done, run the QA for the whole nation and write a short report to `<RESULTS>/_dispatch/reports/<date>_{NATION}_report.md` (per-sign table; tokens and wall-clock; rule breaks and fixes; the thin, repetitive or generic sheets by name; pointers per sheet; whether the sheets exploit the nation's depth). Touch only this nation's Springboards folders, Run log lines (append only) and that report; do not edit the first line of SPRINGBOARD_PROGRESS.md.

## SCHEDULE from the author (2026-11-15, evening): start with the Mirny Subnet's nations
Chosen by the Mirny Subnet's cities (see `Representation by subnet and city (draft).md`). **Korea is done.** Schedule (author's words, one nation-day each; the status script resumes anything cut off):
- **Day 1 (Mon 2026-11-16): Japan, then Australia, then Mongolia.**
- **Days 2, 3 and 4: the post-Russia countries**, three days at 4 a day (author, 2026-11-15: twelve is too many for two). The twelve targets in `Targets/Russia/`, proposed grouping: **Day 2:** Russia (core state), Künnarantaiga, Idelsk-Uralia, Donska-Kubania. **Day 3:** Siberia, Tuva, Chechnya, Dagestan. **Day 4:** Ingushetia, Kabardino-Balkaria, Karachay-Cherkessia, North Ossetia-Alania. (Finland is also a Russia successor in the census table but sits with the European nations; confirm with the author.)
- **Days 5 and 6: the Sinian Federation, sub-divided by subregion** (regional Chinese cultures vary widely: "what kind of Sinian"). Sub-units = the **nine Han member states**, set up as tracker stubs at `Targets/Asia/East Asia/Sinian Federation/Subregions/<name>/`: Zhongyuan 中原 (Beijing, Tianjin, Hebei, Shandong, Henan), Qin 秦 (Shaanxi, Gansu, Ningxia, Qinghai), Bashu 巴蜀 (Sichuan, Chongqing, Hubei, Yunnan, Guizhou), Jin 晋 (Shanxi), Wu 吴 (Shanghai, Zhejiang, Jiangsu, Anhui), Gan 赣 (Jiangxi), Xiang 湘 (Hunan), Min 闽 (Fujian, Hainan), Yue 粤 (Guangdong, Guangxi, Hong Kong, Macau). Each runs as its own "nation" (NATION = the member state's name; the status and QA scripts find it by its tracker). About 4 to 5 a day. **Hakka** (about 44 million, non-territorial): added 2026-11-17 by the author as a **tenth sub-unit**, tracker at `Targets/Asia/East Asia/Sinian Federation/Subregions/Hakka/`; written for the Hakka people and communities, not a territory. Not this week: scheduled for next week.
- **Then:** review where the week stands and schedule the remaining nations. Mirny nations not yet scheduled: New Zealand (8 of 9 cities), then Taiwan, Kazakhstan, Thailand, Vietnam, Patani, Aceh, Indonesia, West Papua, Finland (7 of 9).
- **Cost note:** about 24 nation-runs over these six days (3 + 12 + 9); at the earlier 1.25 to 1.5% each, about 30 to 36% of the weekly allotment, more if the deeper brief costs a third more. Read the meter before and after the first day and adjust.

## Run window, weekly reset and cost ledger (author, 2026-11-15)
- **Weekly allotment resets every Saturday at 23:00.** This week's days: Mon 11-16 to Sat 11-21 (6 days); the next week runs Sat 23:00 11-21 to Sat 23:00 11-28. **Unspent allotment is lost at the reset**, so if there is slack by Friday or Saturday, pull nations forward from next week (keep a safety margin).
- **Run window: 05:00 to 15:00**, the usual hours, the same as the ULM's. **Wrap-up from about 14:00:** launch no new nation after about 14:00 unless it finishes before 15:00; whatever is in flight finishes, and the status script resumes the rest next morning. A nation takes about 66 to 73 minutes, and nations run in parallel (one run agent each), so a day's batch fits in the window; stagger the launches if the number of parallel agents causes trouble.
- **The meter is unreliable** while the ULM runs in parallel. **Cost is tracked by tokens** instead: each run agent's report gives its sub-agent tokens, and the main session adds the run agent's own tokens. Calibration so far: about 1.1 to 1.35 million tokens per 1% of the weekly allotment (Sonora and Korea, the author's readings). The ULM's share is the meter movement minus the DCI tokens at that rate.
- **Total still to run:** 88 runs after Korea, because the Sinian Federation counts as nine (its member states): 3 + 12 + 9 this week = 24, then **65** next week (64 plus Hakka, added 2026-11-17) (9 to 10 a day if all 7 days are used).

- 2026-11-18 (author's ruling) ORDER AFTER THE SINIAN FEDERATION: by subnet, in this order: Mirny, Janbogo, Halley, Palmer, Mawson, Byrd. Each subnet takes the not-yet-done nations represented in its cities (from `Representation by subnet and city (draft).md`), strongest Mirny presence first (Primary 3, Significant 2, Notable 1, summed over the subnet's cities). Amundsen-Scott is a special case, to be sorted out later. Pace (author): 12 nations a day in waves of 4, from Sun 11-22. OFFICIAL plan (author, 2026-11-18; full procedure in `_dispatch/WEEK_2026-11-22_SCHEDULE.md`) (74 runs), by subnet in the author's order (day and wave in brackets):
  - Sinian Federation (10): [Sun 22, wave 1] Zhongyuan, Qin, Bashu, Jin; [Sun 22, wave 2] Wu, Gan, Xiang, Min; [Sun 22, wave 3] Yue, Hakka
  - Mirny (47): [Sun 22, wave 3] Indonesia, New Zealand; [Mon 23, w1] Taiwan, Vietnam, Germany, Philippines; [Mon 23, w2] Thailand, Kazakhstan, United Kingdom, Malaysia; [Mon 23, w3] South Africa, West Papua, Aceh, Patani; [Tue 24, w1] Finland, CSA, Manchuria, East Turkestan; [Tue 24, w2] Tibet, Austro-Bavaria, France, Belarus; [Tue 24, w3] Appalachia, New England, Bulgaria, Lithuania; [Wed 25, w1] Estonia, Latvia, Hungary, Quebec; [Wed 25, w2] Midwest Republic, Colorado, Romania, Chile; [Wed 25, w3] Northern Ireland, Hawaii, Ukraine, DMZ; [Thu 26, w1] Prairie Federation, Meridia, Magna Lombardia, Araucanía-Patagonia; [Thu 26, w2] Argentina, Sweden, Norway, Spain; [Thu 26, w3] Netherlands, Sicilia, Serbia, Croatia
  - Janbogo (11): [Fri 27, w1] Czechia, Poland, México, Slovakia; [Fri 27, w2] Slovenia, Catalonia, Yucatán, Basque Country
  - Halley (5): [Fri 27, w3] Brazil, Amazonia, Uruguay, Denmark; [Sat 28, w1] Iceland
  - Palmer, Mawson, Byrd (0): every nation in their cities is covered earlier.
  - No subnet (1): [Sat 28, w1] Greenland
  Mirny's cities hold 47 of the 64 remaining nations, Janbogo adds 11, Halley 5; Palmer, Mawson and Byrd add none (all their nations come earlier); Greenland is in no city. Budget: 18% a day at 1.5% per nation; one window holds about 5 days of this pace, so the plan depends on the weekly reset day (unconfirmed).
